ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u7	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! The government of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki places great emphasis on the development of modern, advanced technologies, including those related to the hydrogen economy. Since the Ministry of Climate was established, i.e. Since November 2019, we have started work on both the Polish hydrogen strategy and a number of implementing documents for this strategy paper. The work, advanced consultations in expert groups lasted for many months, and then the whole legislative process related to the opinion of this document, the Polish hydrogen strategy took place. And so at the beginning of November this year. The Council of Ministers adopted a resolution on the Polish hydrogen strategy by 2030 with a view to 2040. It is a strategic document setting out the main objectives of the development of the hydrogen economy in Poland and actions to achieve them. The document is part of global and European efforts to build a low-carbon economy. The vision and overarching goal of the Polish hydrogen strategy is to create a new sector of the Polish economy, which will be part of the European and global economy. It is a hydrogen economy and its development to achieve climate neutrality and improve the competitiveness of the economy is extremely important. The Member asked for a reduction in emissions. It fits into this trend, but the key to thinking, is to create greater chances of competitiveness, even more: expansion, Polish economy in the course of work with groups of experts, with Polish research institutes such as the Institute of Energy, the Institute of Petroleum and Gas in Krakow, where there is the largest, most advanced hydrogen laboratory in Poland, one of the largest in Europe. We have developed a number of directional actions that are about to lead to improving the efficiency and competitiveness of the economy. And so the main objectives of the Polish hydrogen strategy are: the implementation of hydrogen technologies in energy and heating, the use of hydrogen as an alternative fuel in transport. The question was right to refer to different modes of transport, but mainly to heavy transport, freight transport, both wheeled and rail. Work is already underway, among others. Bydgoszcz PESA or Alstom Polska are working on prototypes of hydrogen drives for railway, for locomotives. They are already being tested, but we want to work with the best in Europe and in the world. Our main task is to raise local content, i.e. to maximise the participation of Polish entrepreneurs, Polish technological thought throughout the value chain. Therefore, even if the technologies currently being tested and used in this area are imported technologies, we are convinced that in a few years' time, those conditions created by the government, which also the Ministry of Climate and Environment is working on, will result in a large part - we assumed that after 2030 it would be more than 50% throughout the value chain - they will be Polish components. It is both the participation of Polish business, industry and science and technological thought, all institutions, including those from the business environment and, of course, supporting it in a sustainable way of government administration, which is obliged to provide a stable regulatory environment in this area, in cooperation with the Polish parliament. The next steps we take in this direction are extremely important, namely the world's first hydrogen sector deal. Sector deal, i.e. sectoral agreement of the whole industry, the whole sector, starting with large companies, so-called. Anchor around which we will build a value chain, which will provide how many entities will cooperate, cooperate with large entities such as Orlen, Orlen South, where the first hydrogen hub in Poland has already been opened - recently, last month, also with entities such as KGHM Polska Miedź SA or Grupa Azota, today the largest hydrogen producer in Europe. Well, High Izbo, Poland is today the third producer of hydrogen in the European Union and the fifth producer of hydrogen in the world. It's a commitment position. Of course, it is hydrogen produced in emission technologies, while in the coming years both all activities resulting from the Polish hydrogen strategy, as well as building a sectoral agreement involving representatives of the best Polish universities, research and development institutes, but also, as I said, mainly industry, business, smaller companies that will cooperate, organizations from the business environment and government administration... At present 156 entities have joined this agreement. The formula is still open, and in the near future we will be setting up a coordination board consisting of 21 people, representatives of the entire sector. All in order to intensify work on the construction of the Polish hydrogen economy (Dawn) and so that this process is not accidental, that it is properly managed, skillfully stimulated by the government. Thank you.

I will remind you that according to the new tax regulations part of the Polish Government did not change the rules of transfer 1% tax, it can still be done. I will also remind you that, in accordance with the regulations in force, in particular due to the time of the COVID pandemic, more time has been given to taxpayers to settle 1%. This can be done on the basis of statements, corrections, PIT-OP statements made one month later in relation to income tax settlements made in pre-pandemic conditions. Automatic transfer of 1% has also been introduced in the absence of a declaration. This is the situation of pensioners and pensioners who are not required to testify. Transmission 1% is carried out through the tax authority's system on the basis of the data that were in the accounts in previous years. In this case, this amount is 1% the tax goes to the organisation previously indicated by the taxable person in the declaration. I would like to remind you that from the beginning of this institution, a total of more than PLN 8.5 billion has already been transferred to these institutions. In 2021 it was 972 700 000. zł, according to the data on 15 September this year. As part of the Polish Load, about 18 million Poles will actually benefit from this tax change. In view of the consequences, tax cuts for around 2/3 of taxpayers and the total lack of taxation for 1/3 of taxpayers - that is almost 9 million people - it must be noted that there may be a change in the structure of the financing of tax organisations. In this respect, we are the Ministry of Finance on an ongoing basis in contact with public benefit organisations that send their comments to the Ministry in connection with accepted tax changes. We also cooperate closely with the Council of Public Use Activities in KPRM, the Ministry of Finance is a member there. We are open as the Ministry of Finance to take into account the moment of this change - the effects of which, I remind you, will not be visible in 2022, only in 2023, after the settlement of PIT for... [[It's okay.]] Yes, of course, my fellow Member. ...in 2022 - starting from the first quarter of next year a dialogue with public benefit organisations so as to assess, in cooperation with the NAP, the real impact of these changes on the practice of public benefit organisations, the method of financing and together, also in the form of dialogue, the legislative proposals for the future, which could start to apply from the beginning of 2023, i.e. when this effect would occur. Their shape, we hope, will be the result of joint analyses, workshop work, so that at this stage, when we have not yet designed these solutions, it is difficult to talk about concreteities and indicate the nature and scope of these changes. As regards support for persons with disabilities, on the one hand, it is possible for each taxpayer to make a donation and for those who wish to deduct this tax donation, this possibility exists in the legislation, which has not been changed. In contrast, assistance to persons with disabilities is also provided for in the tax system in the form of relief for persons with disabilities. That's what I call it. rehabilitation allowance - it consists of deduction from the income of rehabilitation expenditure, expenditure relating to the facilitation of life activities which are incurred in a tax year by a taxable person who is a disabled person or a taxable person whose dependants are persons with disabilities. This expenditure catalogue is specified in the PIT Act. More than a million taxpayers each year benefit from this relief. It is worth recalling that the Polish Łada introduced changes in rehabilitation relief. Taxpayers will for the first time be entitled to deduct from income expenses incurred on diapers, diapers, anatomical diapers, absorbent panties, primers, anatomical contributions of an amount not exceeding PLN 2280 in the tax year, including for the purchase, repair and rental of medical devices. Currently, this allowance entitles you to deduct expenses incurred for the purchase and repair of individual equipment, equipment and technical tools necessary for rehabilitation and to facilitate the performance of life activities according to the needs arising from disability, except for household appliances. By means of the proposed deduction, the taxable person's expenses will also be incurred for the rental of such equipment, equipment, technical tools and expenditure on the purchase, repair or rental of equipment enabling the use of such items. As far as the caretaker's stay on the rehabilitation tournament is concerned, so far we have only been able to deduct the expenses of people with disabilities themselves incurred on the tournament. The new rules will make it possible to deduct expenses for the stay of the caretaker with a disabled person. [[♪ The bell ♪]] It will also be possible to deduct all expenses incurred for the carriage of a disabled person, not just those necessary for medical and rehabilitation treatments. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u21	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mrs. Member! The reasons why public benefit organisations do not receive information from the tax office are the centralisation of the bank account and the centralisation of tax settlements that took place in 2020. The taxpayer's micro-accounts have been introduced. Centralisation is a consequence of the transformation of the National Tax Administration, introducing more modern solutions. Information on the taxable person's data which transfers 1% a tax for a public benefit organisation shall be available if that taxable person agrees to its publication. The taxpayer does not consent in all cases. However, there is really no information about a specific tax office. I would like to inform you that, as part of the changes in the organisation and functioning of the National Tax Administration, we are introducing - this is related to the facilitation and re-bureaucratisation of the activities of the National Tax Administration - the location of tax activities. The taxpayer will not be associated with a specific tax office. We plan to implement such functionalities next year. The taxpayer will be able to make a declaration at any place in Poland, regardless of the place of residence or business activity. Hence, there is no way to return to a taxpayer's attachment to a specific tax office. On the other hand, in quotes, losses due to contributions to a public benefit organisation are most often linked to a situation where a taxable person does not pay the tax due in the prescribed time limit. The deposit is two months from when the statement should be filed. In fact, a number of cases where funds for the organisation of public benefit have not been transferred are those where the taxpayer simply did not pay the tax due. Much less was there a situation where no updates were made or information was given regarding the number of the public benefit organisation's account. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u25	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mrs. Member! All those who are interested in collective transport! I am very pleased that we can once again talk about this at the Sejm and the Sejm committees, because there is indeed something to talk about. I'll start by saying that the collective transport bill is not the only law that regulates this area. It was actually adopted in 2010 and has been operating in its main scope since then, but it did not provide real support to local authorities as regards the organisation of collective transport. It does not bring any tangible financial advantage, any possible financial support as regards the organisation of collective transport at the level of the commune, district or voivodship. The Government of Law and Justice has noticed this problem. Since mid- 2019 there has been a law which directly supports the organisation of collective transport, this road. I'm talking about the bill that set up the Bus Transport Development Fund. Under this law, first 1 PLN to the car km, and from March 2020 as much as 3 PLN to the car km is transferred to every organizer who wants to create a public utility line, who submitted an application to the regional office, to every local government, district government, voivodeship or association of these local authorities. And this is the real support that we have been providing since 2019. We abstained from legislation that would amend the law on collective transport, because, first, we have been dealing with the COVID-19 pandemic since March, and thus with much less mobility of the Polish society, with much less interest in collective transport. We had to focus at that time on making it even stronger, above all financially supporting the organisation of this transport, and we have done so. The fact that we increased the amount of the surcharge to the car from 1 PLN to 3 PLN is one thing. The second point is that we have created a refund mechanism for reduced tickets used in bus transport. This refund takes place in respect of 2019, i.e. the time when transport was not limited to anything, was not limited by pandemics or administrative decisions. During the pandemic, there have been restrictions on the number of passengers who can travel collectively, although this has not been the case since June this year. Although we will soon introduce limits in cultural institutions and in many other institutions, collective transport will not be covered by this restriction, because we know that it is used to reach school, to work and to move especially to those people who do not have such individual means of transport. In conclusion, I would like to say that in our view, but also in the opinion of the organisers, this most important law, which in a concrete and measurable way supports the organisation of collective transport, is the act on the bus transport fund. In 2022, support will continue to be available in the form of a maximum of PLN 3 per tonne-kilometre. We have managed to make legal changes that have already entered into force, and they are already in force. The Voivodeships can already announce calls for organizers, only... Exactly, we need even greater interest in local governments. And what I am calling for is to draw the attention of the local authorities that, under the Law of 2010 on collective transport, they are the organisers of collective transport, and that is their responsibility. We want to help them with this, we are giving them a specific financial offer: a surcharge of PLN 3 to the carzokm, we help to ensure that the discount refund is at the 2019 level, i.e. at a level higher than in 2020 and 2021, but it is their responsibility to organise this transport. We in Warsaw will not create bus lines somewhere in Poland and I think that none of us here will take it. For this there are local governments, because local governments know local needs from the municipal level to the voivodship level. It is the councilmen, the uncles, the mayors, the elders, the presidents who should carry out this duty. Why? Because - I am convinced - they would do it better than we would from Warsaw. They know their territory, they know their community, they know what migrations look like, how people move, and how to organize these public utility lines. So, once again, I stress: 3 zlotys of subsidies to cars kilometres under the Bus Fund Act in 2022 will be maintained. We encourage local governments to use this tool to create public utility lines. This year we've been dealing with the creation of over 4,000. [[♪ The bell ♪]] public utility lines all over Poland, exactly 4081 communication lines. We hope that this incremental trend will be maintained in the coming years. Thank you very much.

Engineering units built 195 km of a horizontal barrier, as well as 181 km of a vertical engineering barrier, that is, this fence, which is set at the border. In addition, since September 2, we have also built a protection for the game, protecting the game so that it does not undergo mutilation. So we're also building the so-called. Soldiers receive five meals each day: breakfast, second breakfast, lunch, dinner as well as a fifth meal in the form of a regenerative night meal. Most often it's hot soup, which is delivered to the places where there are police stations, where soldiers serve at these night posts. In addition, as I mentioned, these food rations have been strengthened so that soldiers can also receive additional meals, if necessary, with the possibility of preparing an additional warm meal in the so-called food formula. instant, ready meals, prepared soups, but also receive bars, energy gels, as well as isotonic cocktails. This is what soldiers receive in addition in their food rations. So this food is prepared really very well and with care for the soldiers. The macro-region, as you have already mentioned, is, above all, very diverse, but also characterised by low transport accessibility both internally and externally, low availability of public services. The decapitalisation of public and strategic infrastructure as well as a low level of entrepreneurship and innovation is also observed. This, in turn, worsens investment conditions, affects the productivity of local economies, also has a significant impact on the reduction in the quality of life of residents and on the significant outflow of residents, especially young people, entrepreneurs, educated, who are looking for a better life in other regions, not necessarily in the macro-region of Eastern Poland. Recent years have shown the effectiveness of investments in funds and recapitalisation of various areas under the Eastern Poland programme, which also translates into indicators, because the indicators of the level of socio-economic development were the strongest in Eastern Poland regions: from 25% of the overpaid distance to the EU average in the Lublin Voivodeship to 42% in the Warmia-Mazurskie voivodship. ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u75	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Indeed, medical rescue from the beginning, the outbreak of the COVID-19 pandemic is on the front line. Once again, I thank all the employees of the State Medical Rescue: doctors, nurses, rescuers, because it really rested their shoulders and they are still fighting on this first line against the invasion of COVID-19. During each wave, certain measures have been taken to ensure that State Medical Rescue meets new challenges. Therefore, a number of actions that have already worked out during previous waves are also being applied at the moment. I'm talking about the simplest actions, for example. to alleviate certain requirements, for example, for the heads of ambulance teams P, for medical emergency teams that ride with a doctor. If this doctor is absorbed and works e.g. In the SOR, this team can drive without a doctor, of course with an extra lifeguard. Also for medical dispatchers, certain requirements have been relaxed, but, of course, we still make sure that the quality of the benefits is at the right level. We have also extended the medical emergency clearances so that they can download material from the upper respiratory tract, which is the test material for Coronavirus. At the moment, every patient who is taken by a medical emergency team has a swab taken, has a coronavirus test. As was the case during previous waves, voivodeships can also contract additional medical rescue teams, so that in places where there is an increased number of new calls, where there are more of these calls, there are definitely more ambulances. During the previous waves, more than 160 ambulances were still contracted. For this we have allocated over PLN 42 million. We've also put in place a lot of IT systems so the medical dispatcher knows where the coronavirus patient is going to go to avoid queues under hospital emergency services sometimes. Of course, this is not always avoidable, but at the moment we have a database of free beds and according to this dispatcher directs a given ambulance with a patient. We also increased the funding of medical rescue teams. Only for the last quarter of this year have we allocated an additional 129 million PLN. This is an increased funding because the medical technology assessment agency has increased the daily rescue team's lump sum. We also introduced a 30% supplement, so-called. away, for workers who are active in medical rescue at the moment. This was also the result of an agreement between the Minister of Health and the Protest Committee of Medical Rescuers and the Association of Medical Rescue Employers. From 1 October the valuation of the so-called. extracartes, i.e. a daily lump sum, S syndrome, or a team with a doctor, that is 6121 zł, and P team - 4590 zł. For next year, the total expenditure on medical rescue teams will be PLN 2847 million, i.e. 20% more than in the current year. If we compare this historically, 2007 to 2022, this is an increase in medical rescue funding by 266%, which is a very large leap. The exit allowance, which is paid to administrators and handed over to emergency workers, has also already been legalized and is now paid to all emergency workers or to the entire team that participates in the action. We have also made many investments in medical rescue. In 2019 we allocated 80 million from the state budget for the purchase of 200 ambulances. This programme continued in the following years and in 2020 we allocated 58 million. That's what another 97 ambulances were purchased from. In 2021, i.e. present, we allocated 58 million and bought 112 ambulances, plus 12 ambulances for the Silesian province from a separate program. In total, we have allocated over PLN 210 million to exchange ambulances. We also allocated PLN 1600 million from the EU's infrastructure and environment programme in 2014 and 202021. During the pandemic, medical rescue teams received a COVID supplement, as did other workers who were directly involved in the fight against COVID. In addition, since August, a team has been operating in the Ministry of Health - its work is also attended by representatives of the medical rescue community - working on amending the Act on State Medical Rescue. We want the Law (The Bell), which was adopted in 2006, to be remodeled at the moment, because in many respects it no longer meets the real needs. Finally, work on the Law on the Occupation and Local Government of Medical Rescuers is being completed, and soon it will come into force. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u82	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, This is a very important aspect in the fight against COVID-19. Indeed, the transition to coronavirus infection causes the need for rehabilitation in many patients. At the moment, it is available in the country for both adults and patients under 18 years of age. In order to obtain such rehabilitation, it is necessary, of course, to direct the disease COVID-19 after the end of treatment. The referral is issued by a doctor of health insurance, which is actually any doctor who has a contract with the NFZ, e.g. Family doctor. Rehabilitation can be done in two ways: either in fixed or outpatient mode. Patients are mainly treated with COVID-19 complications and consequences. These may be complications associated with the functioning of the respiratory system, the cardiovascular system, the nervous system, the movement organs or a decrease in muscle strength. This is, of course, assessed using a certain scale that is attached to the referral. Each developer of this program, i.e. any facility that is engaged in rehabilitation, must of course have the appropriate staff and equipment. He must perform rehabilitation in accordance with our regulation. The program guarantees rehabilitation lasting from 2 to 6 weeks. Rehabilitation must not be carried out later than 12 months after the end of treatment with COVID-19. As part of rehabilitation, we offer kinesitherapy, taping, chest taping, bed drains, individual inhalations, oxygen therapy, Balner therapy, therapeutic massage, hydrotherapy, relaxation training, health education, diet therapy, and, of course, psychological care. In addition, each institution participating in the programme provides patients with diagnostic tests, medicines and medical devices that are necessary to achieve the best results of the treatment programme. Domestic rehabilitation is also available. It can be used. In this case, rehabilitation is performed at home. Therapy is quite diverse. Its course depends on the condition of the patient, on the ability of his body. We will not rule out the use of rehabilitation by people under 18 years of age. We're talking about children and young people. Apart from typical spas and stationary rehabilitation wards, rehabilitation of people under the age of 18 also takes place in outpatient mode. It is worth mentioning several examples of facilities: Regional Specialist Hospital in Wrocław, Karpacz Medical Centre, University Children's Hospital in Lublin, Rabka Spa, Regional Specialist Hospital in Krakow, University Children's Hospital in Krakow. These are places where people under the age of 18 are rehabilitated. The National Health Fund has signed 29 agreements with centres that implement rehabilitation benefits for children and young people up to 18 years old. Several dozen underage patients have already benefited from rehabilitation in various voivodships between May and September. Well, maybe it's not much, 'cause he's using rehab. by far more adults, but it is possible for all people under 18, children and young people, to benefit from rehabilitation, if necessary and doctors who take care of people after having been COVID-19, to make such a decision. According to our predictions and simulations of experts, we are already in the vicinity of the fourth wave summit, but of course the number of patients who will require hospitalisation will continue to be quite high for the next few weeks. Therefore, by 10 December, we want the number of beds for patients with COVID-19 to be 32 thousand and ventilators to be more than 2.8 thousand. The most important thing, of course, is for us to vaccinate ourselves. At the moment, we see an increase in the interest of Poles taking the first dose, but also the third booster dose. We can see that this interest is growing overnight. And this is very, very good information, because especially this booster dose, which is dedicated to people over the age of 18, as research shows, strongly increases our immunity and protects us from the effects of COVID-19 infection. We are also preparing to vaccinate children from 5 to 11 years of age at the moment. We look forward to the arrival in Poland of these vaccines dedicated to children from this age range. Maybe a little bit of statistics on people who died who were hospitalized and were vaccinated. I think this will also clearly show that vaccinations work, that vaccinations are needed. The number of infections in Poland since the start of vaccination with the second dose is 2020. So many people got sick. The number of infections among vaccinated subjects 14 days after the second dose is 259 120. The percentage of people who were fully vaccinated but who were ill is 12.8, i.e. 10 infected 9 people were non-injected. If we look at deaths, in the case of deaths in Poland since the second dose in Poland the number of deaths is 47 531. The number of deaths among those fully vaccinated after 14 days is 2960, i.e. 6.3%, or out of 100 deaths, 94 were uninsulated. Recordings show that vaccination protects if we consider the deaths of people who have taken a full two-dose dose have been fully vaccinated. Of course, we must also consider their multi-morbidity, age and so-called. other factors that also favored the fact that these people died. If we look at hospitalizations since the second dose, there were 102 048 hospitalized in Poland. Fully vaccinated, he was hospitalized. 5018, i.e. 4.9% , i.e. out of 100 people who were fully hospitalized, there were 95 people who were not vaccinated. In 2021, 34 300 million are spent on the fight against COVID-19. We are now increasing this financial plan of the COVID fund by another 5 billion. We plan for this pot to be over PLN 21 billion next year. We also allocate EU funds of over PLN 1 billion. There were funds intended for the purchase of ambulances, including personal protective equipment. We've done some training. 373 people have been trained to operate ventilators this year. Last year it was almost 120 people. Over 16,000. physiotherapists and pharmacists have been trained to carry out vaccinations. Over 16,000. persons in this group are qualified for vaccination. We have increased at the moment - if necessary, we are supplementing reserves - resources in terms of ventilators, cardio monitors, infusion pumps, mammals, medical beds, oxygen, oxygen cylinders. These are all investments that are transferred to individual entities that are engaged in the fight against COVID-19. Let me just remind you that over 7,000 have been handed over from the Material Reserve Agency. respirators, 25 CTs, 115 digital X-rays, 2.8 thousands. High-flow therapy apparatus, 240 EKG cameras, 85 ultrasound cameras, over 7,000. Hospital beds, 13,000. cardiomonitors and many, many millions of other things, such as protective masks - over 700 million, protective suits - over 6 million, disinfectant fluids - 25 million l. These are really very large volumes that have been designed to fight the coronavirus. But the situation isn't standing still. As you certainly know, a new variant of Coronavirus has emerged, the option of Omicron, which is very concerned by many experts who study the variation of Coronavirus on a daily basis. That is why we have taken steps to reduce the expansion of this new option. We are conducting a review of passenger location cards that have arrived in Poland since 15 October this year. We're directing them to molecular tests to detect a possible variant. Health services have also significantly expanded epidemiological intelligence for people who have returned from countries where the Omicron variant exists. All samples from people who return to Poland from the areas where the Omicron variant was found are kept. We have increased the number of weekly samples so that all suspicious samples are subjected to this quite specific diagnostic method. We've got over ten labs all over the country right now. These are the labs that are under the authority of the chief health inspector. These are also laboratories at medical universities. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Today, we are introducing restrictions that will protect us from the possible emergence of this new variant of Coronavirus. As of today, the 14-day quarantine has been extended to all those returning from these countries in South Africa. This quarantine will be here regardless of whether it is positive or not, this quarantine is mandatory. There will also be a 14-day quarantine for travellers outside the Schengen area. She is extended, but with the possibility of performing tests after the 8th day and in the event of a negative result from such quarantine, these persons will be released. As of today, there have also been sharpenings in many institutions, commercial facilities, gyms, fitness clubs, including gatherings, catering facilities and hotels. These are all those actions that precede the possible emergence of a new variant of Coronavirus in Poland. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u204	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Sejm Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, I have the honour to present a report on the government draft law amending certain laws in connection with the extension of the implementation of the Rural Development Programme for the 2014-2020 period, print No 1775. The project was very widely consulted, among others. with trade union organisations, employers' organisations, the Joint Committee on Government and Local Government, the Council of Social Dialogue, the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, the National Agricultural Support Centre, research institutes, social organisations, as well as local governments of voivodships. Most of the comments submitted were taken into account in the project under investigation. The proposed amendments involve an extension of the implementation of the Rural Development Programme for the period 2014 to 2020 in accordance with the provisions of Regulation (EU) 2020/2220 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 23 December 2020. The need to extend the implementation of the RDPs is due to the fact that the legislative procedure relating to the European Commission's legislative proposals for the common agricultural policy after 2020 has not been completed in time to enable Member States and the European Commission to prepare all the elements necessary for the application of the new legal standards and strategic plans of the common agricultural policy. Consequently, the transitional Regulation ensured that the provisions of the current CAP framework covering the period 2014 to 2020 and that payments to farmers and other beneficiaries continue to apply, which guarantees predictability and stability during the transitional period for 2021 and 2022, until the date of application of the new legal framework covering the period beginning on 1 January 2023. During the work of the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, the project was discussed. No substantive amendments were tabled. The project was accepted by all members of the Commission. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development welcomes the proposal submitted by the government to amend certain laws in connection with the extension of the programme. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u240	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present a position regarding the draft Print Act No 1778 amending the Public Procurement Law and certain other laws. This draft law is the result of the experience of the Ministry of National Defence in the practical application of the article. 4 point 5b in conjunction with Article. 4c Law of 29 January 2004 - Public procurement law. Analyses resulting from several years of application of the regulations on military equipment procurement may be considered as special equipment designed or adapted for military use and intended for use as weapons, ammunition or war material, obtained for the sole interest of State security, indicate the appropriateness of modifying the provisions in order to simplify, implement and adapt to the requirements of Union law. It is extremely important to note that, under EU law, the content of the Article. EC Treaty and Article 296 EC. 346 TFEU has not changed since 25 May 2006. There was only a transfer of the same content from the editorial unit of the article. 296 TEC to the new editorial unit - Art. 346 TFEU as a result of changes to the Treaty. At the level of EU law, therefore, the substantive continuity of regulation has been maintained despite changes to the Treaties. Since 1 January 2021, public procurement law has entered into force. The analysis of the new law shows that it responds to the long-term expectations of both public entities and economic operators applying for public contracts. More than 14 years of public procurement law, numerous amendments to these provisions as a result of the transposition of EU directives and changes to adapt the law to the needs of changing legal and economic situations have generated the need to prepare a new, comprehensive and coherent public procurement law. The aim of the bill is to introduce new solutions based on the assumption of maximum efficiency and transparency of public procurement, while taking into account the role of public procurement in shaping state policy and the need to support the development of small and medium-sized enterprises as well as innovative and modern products and services. It has also become necessary to increase the transparency and consistency of public procurement regulations. Most importantly, the preparation of the new law was not the direct result of the transposition of EU law into the Polish legal system, because these were already incorporated into the law in previous years. The new legislative delegation contained in the article. 15 Public procurement law requires that a much wider range of circumstances be taken into account, including the procedure for assessing the existence of a fundamental interest in State security, the way in which measures to protect that interest are defined, the proportionality of the measures used to ensure the protection of that interest and those competent for the classification of tasks as essential to the security of the State, the way in which the scope of application in the procurement procedure of the law is determined, taking into account the obligation to ensure the correct application of the provision of the article. 346 Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union and the need to ensure the security of supplies of military equipment throughout the cycle. In these circumstances, the repealed provisions appear as a kind of overregulation resulting from misinterpretation of EU law rules, which seeks to regulate rigidly in national law issues which have not been established at the level of Union law, and which Member States have also not been required to do in EU law. The conclusions resulting from the interpretation of Article should be recognised and respected. 346 on the basis of public procurement, which must be taken into account by the contracting authority irrespective of the repealed provisions of the Public Procurement Law. Further stiffening and limiting the applicability of the Article is not justified. 346 TFEU in public procurement by means of regulation of national law generally in force. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The proposed regulations will have no legal impact, but they can have a positive effect on social, economic and financial impacts. Therefore, the Law and Justice Club will vote in favour of the bill submitted. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u244	Dear Marshal! Dear Minister, High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the parliamentary club of the Left regarding the parliamentary draft law amending the law - Public Procurement Law and some other laws, print No 1778. First of all, I would like to remind you why we actually have public procurement law in our legal order. I'm going to explain this first on a micro scale, and then I'm going to go to a macro scale. In every company, the owner wants the company's employees to manage the assets he has entrusted to them. The purchasing departments in companies play a particular role because employees make decisions that affect the finances of the company and the quality of the goods and services produced. In extreme cases, irresponsible purchasing department could lead to bankruptcy of the company. Therefore, the owners and directors of companies introduce purchasing procedures so that the whole process is transparent and meets the quality and financial requirements. On a macro scale, it looks exactly the same. State units, public institutions, state companies make many purchases, manage the huge wealth that belongs to us, to citizens and to citizens. Shopping money comes from our taxes. We are the owners of this property and we want to be sure that public money will be spent transparently and will contribute to a good quality of public services. We're going to look at what's in the bill now. Applicants, i.e. a group of members of the Law and Justice, wish to simplify purchasing procedures for the production or trade of arms, ammunition or war material, if required by the fundamental interests of state security. The applicants want to delete the paragraph which currently states that in the case of such contracts the contracting authority may apply the provisions of the Law - Public procurement law to the extent specified in the assessment of the existence of the basic interests of State security. This is optional. Until now, the contracting authority could apply the provisions of the Act - Public Procurement Law, but did not have to. The key to the exemption was to assess the existence of a fundamental interest in State security. Art. The 15th Act states that the Council of Ministers shall, by means of a regulation on a proposal from the Minister for National Defence and the Minister for Home Affairs, in agreement with the Minister for Foreign Affairs and the Minister for Economic Affairs, determine the procedure for dealing with the assessment of the existence of the essential security interests of the State, the manner in which measures ensuring the protection of that interest are defined, the proportionality of the measures used to ensure the protection of that interest and the entity competent for the qualification of tasks as essential to the security interests of the State, and the way in which the scope of the procurement procedure is determined. From the taxpayer's point of view, this provision is as appropriate as possible. It makes purchases that are not strategically important from the point of view of state security subject to greater social control. So what are the arguments of the project applicants? The explanatory memorandum of the law states that the main reason is the security of the state. For example, the eastern border and the need for rapid response were established. The full agreement is that it is necessary to react quickly and to ensure the security of the state, only that this argument has nothing to do with the draft law, because the present law already gives such an opportunity. In crisis situations, shopping can be simplified, only the operational need needs need to be justified. The second argument of the project applicants concerned the simplification of purchasing procedures. We read in the explanatory memorandum that this is due to the experience of the defence department, which claims that procedures are overly formalised. But the procedure is just to follow them. The procedures are designed to protect the public interest and not the interests of the contracting authority. In conclusion, I would like to say that this bill is a gateway to abuse. This does not mean that the contracting authority will always use this gate, but some of the unfair contracting authorities, as well as lobbyists, will open it to the public. I don't trust that power. I did not trust Minister Antoni Macierewicz or his associate Misiewicz, I do not trust Minister Błaszczak or Minister Kamiński (Dzwonek), so how can I have confidence in the intention of this bill? The Left will vote against this bill. [[Applause]]

However, on the basis of Article. 15 of the same law The Council of Ministers, by means of a regulation, sets out, inter alia, the procedure for assessing the existence of a basic State security interest. This means that, on the one hand, the rules allow for some flexibility that is needed in the field of military procurement, but on the other hand, they need real analysis. This is the key: the contract fulfils the conditions of the existence of a basic State security interest and should therefore be implemented without application of the Public Procurement Law. ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u280	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Physical Culture, Sports and Tourism, I am pleased to present a report on the government draft law amending the act on combating doping in sport, print no. 1788. Marshal of the Sejm, according to Art. Article 37 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 and Article 1 Article 40 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, after consulting the Sejm Bureau, sent the above draft law to the Committee on Physical Culture, Sport and Tourism for first reading on 26 November 2021. The bill results from the need to adapt Polish legal standards governing the functioning of the Polish Anti-Doping Laboratory, PLAD, to the international standard for laboratories, ISL 2021, as amended by the World Anti-Doping Agency in September 2020. There are currently no national provisions necessary for the correct application of the ISL 2021 from 1 January 2022. The new wording of the standard assumes that an anti-doping laboratory accredited by WADA cannot be managed by a national anti-doping agency, a sports organisation, a Ministry responsible in the country for sport, subject to them or be part of them. Accredited by WAD PLAD is a state legal person and operates under the law. According to Art. Article 47a (1) (a) (ii) (ii) (ii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii)) (ii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii)) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) (iii) It is therefore necessary to amend the Minister currently supervising the laboratory and the provisions resulting from the exercise of assigned supervision. ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u284	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the pleasure on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Civic Coalition - Civic Platform to present the position of the club in relation to the amendment of the act on combating doping in sport. The amendment of the Act follows the amendment of the international standard for laboratories adopted in September 2020 by the World Anti-Doping Agency, WADA. According to this standard, the Polish rules should be adapted to the changes that are taking place. The project adapts the rules in force in Poland to the standards adopted by the international standard for laboratories, because according to the standard accredited by WADA an anti-doping laboratory cannot be managed by a national anti-doping agency, a sports organisation, a Ministry responsible for sport or another government body responsible for sport, subject to them or be part of them. Therefore, as part of the bill's novel, there is a change of entity which will supervise the Polish Anti-Doping Laboratory. According to the WADA standard, national accredited anti-doping laboratories are to be independent and States are required to ensure this independence by 1 January 2022. The new subject will be the Ministry of Health. This supervision has so far been carried out by the Ministry of Physical Culture, Tourism and Sport, the Ministry of National Heritage, and currently by the Ministry of Sport and Tourism. High Izbo! Sports are primarily fair play activities, they are pure actions. The novel is going in the right direction. Doping is a great evil in sport. We should all work together to ensure that sport is clean. We all want to believe that the fight against doping in Polish sport is a priority of the government, not just the declarations made. Therefore, I will ask: Why was the amendment only made to the Sejm after 14 months? After all, the World Anti-Doping Agency's rules have been in force since 20 September 2020? The legislators at the committee meeting yesterday questioned this deadline, justifying that for government projects it should be 30 plus 21, or 51 days. The new rules are due to apply from 1 January, it is a short period of time. Why did the Ministry wait so long to introduce them? At the moment, the threat that we will not be able to adapt to the deadlines required by WADA. Since 1 January 2020, the former Minister of Sport Witold Bianka has been the head of the WADA and Anna Krupka has been a member of the Council of the World Anti-Doping Agency Foundation. There are only 32 laboratories all over the world with such accreditation. Our laboratory operates internationally, not only in Poland. They are institutions cooperating with many entities, hospitals, police and prosecutors and carrying out toxicological services for them. ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u312	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The draft amendment of the act on combating doping in sport is due to the need to change the supervisory entity of the Polish Anti-Doping Laboratory, which is currently supervised by the Ministry of Sport and Tourism. Since 1 January 2022, the WADA accredited anti-doping laboratories will not be supervised by the resort responsible for sport in the country concerned. Therefore, following the findings within the government, it was decided that the laboratory would be supervised by the Minister of Health. I would like to thank the Minister of Health for its openness and acceptance of the responsibilities associated with the supervision of the Polish Anti-Doping Laboratory. At the same time, in answer to your Members' questions in this part, I will say: yes, we have agreements. They are finalised, concrete and under these arrangements the laboratory will be supervised by the Ministry of Health. The second problem that solves the project is the need to shorten the period for which the powers of anti-doping controllers are granted by the director of POLADA, the Polish Anti-Doping Agency. According to the new provisions of the WADA, the powers of anti-doping controllers should be conferred for a period of 2 years rather than for a period of 3 years, as indicated by Article. Article 26 (2) (b) 1 act. This provision will therefore be amended accordingly. As you can see, the project provides for little change and is purely technical and adaptable. Therefore, the project should not be controversial. On this occasion, I would like to thank all your Members for their smooth handling of this law. Fighting doping in sport, fighting for pure sport is a priority for our ministry and the Polish government. I am pleased that it is also a priority for your parliamentarians. I also wanted to address the questions raised in connection with the amendment of the law. Let's get the facts straight. Part of the provisions required by WADA has already entered into force on 1 January 2021. The others are the subject of the amendment. As regards the transitional provision, it is more precise. In the view of the government without this provision, the existing certificates would remain valid, but in order to cut back any doubts, we have decided to support this amendment. As far as real sports spending is concerned, they are larger each year. As I have already stressed, all solutions are agreed between the resorts. [[Why is this project so late?]] Once again, thank you very much for having all of you involved in the fight against doping in sport, fighting for pure sport, because this amendment is an expression of this. Cybercrime knows no borders, which is why in 2016 a directive was adopted on measures for a high common level of cybersecurity across the Union, so-called. NIS. The version of the directive, commonly referred to as the NIS 2 Directive, is now being substantially revised at the end. The new provisions are not only amending by extending the scope of the existing Directive, including: public administrations, not only impose higher management, handling and disclosure requirements, cyber security testing and efficient encryption use. It clarifies and extends incident reporting records. The NIS 2 is also an important new mechanism for international cooperation through the establishment of a European Cyberspace Crisis Management Network. The establishment of an office should take an active part in cooperation with EU Cyclone, a body established for this purpose. That is why I think it is worth introducing provisions into the bill, of course in the near future, which will already correspond with the legislation being implemented, which will make the office a modern unit, which, based on the analysis of incidents of IT systems vulnerability to burglary, will chase criminals faster than they will cover their tracks. Another thing is prevention and exchange of experience. Another element is the amendment of other acts which should be in correlation with this law, such as the National Cyber Security and Crisis Management Act, and together create one common organism, a system that has one task, combat cybercrime and prevent attacks, including at European Union level. On the other hand, the experience of the commander and alternates of the CBZC should be regulated by a regulation. It is undeniable to have an IT education, but I am aware of how the world is changing, there are new courses of study, certification paths in the area of security, so it would be pointless to include precise records in the act outside of IT education and English knowledge. The Minister of the Interior should be required to define the requirements for persons performing such critical functions. This will allow, on the one hand, to follow a changing environment and, on the other hand, to provide a public opinion with requirements regarding the powers of the Central Cybercrime Control Office. ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u370	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! And of course, the esteemed volunteer firefighters, because today we're gonna talk about you in the Seym. I have the great honour of presenting to the High House a report on the work of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on the law on voluntary firefighters. Previously, the Permanent Sub-Committee was involved in this project. the development and promotion of TSOs, followed by the Board of Administration and Home Affairs. The discussion in the administration committee was extremely calm and substantive, for which I would like to thank all the Members who participated in this discussion. Today, it must be recalled - we discussed this in the committee - that the draft law that has been examined by the High House covers the most organized community of our country. It is clear to everyone that there is no safe Poland today without 230 thousand. firefighters volunteer firefighters who account for 96% the total number of fire protection units in our country. At the same time, it is difficult to believe today - we also talked about this in the course of work in committee - that so numerous and well deserved for security Polish formation created by people who for years have been providing unselfish help to another person, has not yet had a dedicated law. The draft law on voluntary firefighters is a long-awaited legal standard, it is a continuation of the joint action of the entire fire community in 1990 and 1991, the aim of which was to reform the legal system from the past era. The result of the actions taken at that time was the adoption by the X-Sejm of the Law on State Fire and Fire Protection. Even then, 22 years ago, at the meeting of the Sejm Sub-Committee in 1991, the then President of the General Board of Volunteer Fire Guards mentioned the need for a law on TSOs. So, ladies and gentlemen, we also discussed this at first reading. The current project is the result of many years of scientific analysis and conferences, the activity of firefighters' friends, professional firefighters and finally the ministry's activities. They all sought to regulate in one legal act the situation of this unique group of people, the people who carry it, once again I will stress this, unselfish help to others. I would like to see this bill, as we are all here, as an expression of great respect for Parliament towards voluntary firefighters. I would like to point out that the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs as a whole - this is the result of the amendments voted - does not share the view which emerged in the discussions in the committees that it is enough to amend the Fire Protection Act. The Fire Protection Act, which should be clearly highlighted, is a system regulation. Voluntary firefighters, so well deserved for state security, should have their own law, as should the State Firefighters. Firefighters volunteered, like professional firefighters, deserved this distinction. It should also be borne in mind that regulations should keep up with the changing challenges around us, especially those relating to the maintenance of personal levels. In the course of the work in committee, the alleged lack of consultation on the draft law was also alleged. Ladies and gentlemen! I really - I was talking about it - I do not remember the draft law, which would be consulted so widely and for a long time. The preliminary project was discussed at many meetings with CSR's friends. I attended several meetings myself. At this point, it should also be recalled that the legislative proposals on the working material were also discussed before the draft law was officially sent to the Sejm, at the meeting of the Permanent Committee for Foreign Affairs. TSOs. This meeting took place on June 30th. The result of these numerous consultations was that many of the demands raised by the fellow Members were included in the bill. The evidence that the firefighters were listening is that this project differs from the original proposal. This is the result of listening to what the bridesmaids said at meetings at this lowest municipal and district level. High Izbo! Turning now from the overall conclusions of the committee's work to the more detailed ones, it must be pointed out that the aim of the proposed regulation is to improve the situation of volunteer firefighters in a thorough manner. For the purpose of this Regulation, the following definitions shall apply: exemption from work, equivalent, compensation, compensation, pensions, discounts, reliefs, and their families also state aid in the event of the death of a volunteer firefighter during rescue operations, which, I hope, will never occur. The lifeguard will be entitled to periodic and free medical examinations as well as free psychological assistance. The firefighter shall be provided with personal protective equipment and special uniforms. Maintaining the firehouses will be less cumbersome by regulating the electricity tariff at household level. Firefighters will be granted a card providing additional privileges. The CSR Firefighters have been given the opportunity to award the Florian Badge for the merits of the Local Community. The State budget provides funding for the training of rescue firefighters of TSOs, including funding for the acquisition of category C driver powers. The rules for the organisation of training of rescue firefighters will allow you to obtain a professional firefighter title. This is just, ladies and gentlemen, some of the benefits of the bill that we discussed during the committee work and earlier in the subcommittee. At the first reading of the subcommittee and the committee, of course, amendments were made. Some of these amendments, tabled by members of the Law and Justice Club, concerned protection of rescue firefighters not only owned by PSPs, but also by residents themselves, the mayor or the mayor. These amendments make it clear that the possibility of leaving the unit can be exercised not only by the PSP, but also by the vodar of the commune or the inhabitant. Here I would like to explain that the shape of the previous provisions in the Act actually allowed firefighters to be protected by this protection, but for the sake of clarity we are detailing these records. Five amendments were adopted at the overall meeting of the Administrative and Home Affairs Committee and 15 minority proposals were also tabled. As far as parliamentarians are concerned, there were 26 votes in favour of the overall bill, along with the amendments adopted, no one voted against and no one abstained. This shows that despite the differences, despite the discussion - fortunately, the discussion, I have the impression, substantive - as a whole of the administration committee, all members of this committee considered that the law on voluntary firefighters is a good law that will serve this environment. I would therefore like, on behalf of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, to recommend that the High House adopt this law together with the amendments. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs at today's meeting, on 1 December 2021, carried out the first reading of the Government's draft law on the establishment of the Police Modernization Programme, Border Guard, State Fire and State Protection Service for the period 2022-2025 and on the amendment of the Police Act and certain other laws. The bill is contained in the Seym printing no. 1789. At the committee meeting there was also an exhaustive, long discussion of the urgent government project presented, and then the project was examined in detail and the vote on its adoption took place at the end. The vote resulted in 27 votes in favour, no abstentions and no votes against. During the committee's decision, provisions were added which were not included in the original submission by means of amendments. The two basic amendments were accepted, quite extensive. 1. Police Andrzej Struja and includes related provisions, especially on an increase of 1% the basis for the pension for the award of this medal. The bill deals with several areas, mainly construction investments, the purchase of equipment for formations supervised by the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration and subordinate to him in the field of transport equipment, arms, special technology, IT and communication, the purchase of personal and protective equipment of officers, the strengthening of permanent officers, the strengthening of the incentive system for the remuneration of officers by increasing the multiple of the basic amount which represents the average salary of officers, the differentiation of the structure and size of the salaries of officers and the increase of the remuneration of the staff of the organizational units of the formation. A large part of the measures provided for under the programme that is being implemented by the Act will be allocated to personal matters, the increase in the number of posts, the increase in the salaries and salaries of officials and employees. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present the position of the club on the draft law establishing the Police Modernization Programme, Border Guard, State Fire and State Protection Service between 2022 and 2025, and on amending the Police Act and certain other laws, printing No. 1789. Ladies and gentlemen! The bill under review assumes that in the perspective of 4 years over PLN 10 billion will be allocated to the programme of modernisation of services. The implementation of the programme is intended to ensure further modernisation of the Police, Border Guard, State Fire and State Protection Service in order to create conditions conducive to their statutory tasks by modernising infrastructure, equipment and equipment. This programme also aims to increase the number of posts and strengthen the incentive system for the remuneration of officials and to increase the remuneration of the staff of organisational units. After two existing service modernisation programmes, it is necessary to continue to implement construction investments, as well as to apply the latest solutions in the area of equipment for transport, weapons and special technology, IT and communication, personal and protective equipment of officers. The need to modernise services in this area there is no need to convince anyone, given the current situation on the Polish-Belarusian border. Another important area of modernisation will be the strengthening of human capital, mainly in terms of substantially increasing motivation, attractiveness, security of service and work, and improving the level of training and qualifications of civil servants and workers by increasing salaries and salaries. The programme also envisages an increase in the development of training, including in particular police schools, in the further modernisation of the teaching and training base and in the improvement of the level and efficiency of education. One of the main objectives of the programme is to continue the process of reactivating the police stations that were wound up in 2008. In total, 477 stations were liquidated during the PO-PSL coalition. The programme will seek to restore or set up 250 police stations. As a reminder, ladies and gentlemen, in the years 2016 and 2020 a total of 132 police stations were reconstructed or established within the framework of the previous multiannual programme. In the field of construction investments modernising the State Fire Department, a total of 120 investment tasks consisting of the construction of 53 guards and the modernization and reconstruction of 67 existing buildings were planned. In addition, it is planned to purchase transport equipment for the operational units of the State Fire Department, necessary to maintain the appropriate operational readiness of the formation. The priority task of modernising the Border Guard is to strengthen the border of the Eastern Republic of Poland, which is at the same time the border of the European Union, by modernising the automated surveillance radar system and building a perimetric border protection system. The contested bill also establishes the.Programme for the modernisation of the Prison Service in the years 2022-2025. Ladies and gentlemen! In conclusion, I would like to say that on behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I declare my support for this law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Additional paragraphs are introduced to provide for the possibility of extraordinary relief of the penalty and even the possibility of withdrawing from it in the event that the child's child's death is caused by his mother. This is intended to make it possible to treat women under pressure and forced to have abortions fairly. As regards punishment for miscarriage, the proposal provides for the exclusion of the possibility of punishing the mother in situations of involuntaryness by using the term: it is not punishable. This exclusion concerns the involuntary form of acts consisting, in principle, of a mother's failure to respect the care required of a person in her condition. So this horrible project disables the possibility of punishing the mother if we are dealing with involuntary guilt. There is also a need to mention the responsibility of doctors. Abortion, or action directed directly to the death of the conceived child, is not a remedy against the risk of the child's health or life. Medicinal effects on the mother may be associated with a risk to the life of the child and sometimes cause his death. In medicine, there are often situations where taking therapy involves a risk to the health or life of the patient. If these actions have been taken according to medical knowledge and art, the doctor will not be liable for the resulting damage. Similarly, if the doctor's actions to save the health or life of the mother were consistent with the medical arts, he is not liable for the harm the child was conceived. Similarly, it is not the responsibility of a doctor whose actions in accordance with medical knowledge, taken to save the health and life of the conceived child, have caused a risk to his health or life or his death. We refer to the explanatory memorandum of the article. 26 Penal Code, existing. We don't want to bring him in because he's here. It does not commit a crime, who acts to lift the direct danger of any good protected by law, if the danger cannot be avoided otherwise, and the deed represents a value less than the good saved. So that the Penal Code contains instruments that protect doctors who act in good faith. On the other hand, they do not protect doctors who, through negligence or lack of knowledge, make medical mistakes that lead to the death of their mother, as was the case in Pszczyna. A few days ago, Minister Niedzielski revealed that the report contained indications that serious medical errors occurred there. The justification for every mother's death because there was no abortion is simply absurd. ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u640	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! November is a dangerous time for Poles. The above words, which are a paraphrase of the statements of one of the heroes of the November Night of Stanisław Wyspiański, perfectly illustrate the events that took place in Warsaw at the end of November 1830. However, before this, it is worth recalling what happened a little earlier. Napoleon Bonaparte remodeled Europe at the beginning of the 19th century. After he left in 1815, it was impossible to return to the rules before the Napoleonic period. This was also true of Polish lands. In 1795 the partition divided our lands between Russia, Prussia and Austria. Napoleon created from their parts a small hull Duchy of Warsaw, promising the restitution of the full Kingdom of Poland. But it didn't. Today we can argue whether Napoleon wanted it or whether it was a political rally. The Emperor's fall has abandoned these plans, but the concept remains. She was returned to the Vienna congress in 1815 when the Russian Tsar Alexander I decided that in a small, controlled area he would form the Constitutional Kingdom of Poland, which he was about to be king. This kingdom was to have a constitution - indeed imposed, but always - and the Sejm and its own army. In the absence of the king, the governor, the former Napoleonic general - General Józef Zabiczek, was to rule in Warsaw. History really likes to play malicious tricks sometimes. Tsar's brother, the great prince of Constantine, became the commander of the army. A little known circumstance is the fact that the famous song of God, something written by Alojze Feliński, was once treated as a song of resistance against the assassins and then the Soviets. At first it may have seemed that this experiment, as she sometimes said about the so-called. The congressional kingdom of the court camel in St. Petersburg, and it worked pretty well. The state was developing economically, the tsar promised to join the so-called kingdom. Lands taken, that is, parts of former eastern voivodships. Everything was as it should have been, but as time went by, the czar and his officials were increasingly breaking the constitution. Resistance to Russian rule was growing in various layers of society. After the death of General Bunny, the brother of Tsar Constantine ruled the state. There have been conspiracies, including in the army, which is a kind of a calling card for this mini-state. Major Valerian Łukasiński was the leader of one. The conspiracy was detected and its leaders were sentenced to a long prison sentence. The tension, however, was increasing. To this day, disputes persist, why the November Uprising actually broke out. His story is, on the one hand, a demonstration, as usual in us Poles, sometimes, of unparalleled bravery and self-sacrifice, and on the other hand, of incompetence, indulgence and mutual personal animosities, which could not be removed despite external danger. The spark of rebellion at the Infantry Sick School in the Bathrooms, which exploded under the command of Lt. Piotr Wysocki on 29 November 1830, there were events related to the great prince. The Podhorążs were running through the streets of Warsaw, shouting: weapons! But the people of the capital turned their backs on them. The underdogs were looking for someone to lead them. In the whirlwind of events, they killed several Polish generals who refused to lead them. If he hadn't joined them 4. The Line Infantry regiment, the uprising probably wouldn't have exploded at all. According to Jerzy Łojek, it was our only national break, next to the successful Wielkopolska Uprising, which had a character about Wielkopolska, which had a real chance of success. High Izbo! 2021 is the year of the 95th anniversary of the sports activities of the Bydgoszcz Rowing Club, the only female rowing club in Poland and Europe. The history of the Bydgoszcz Rowing Club dates back to 1926, when a large number of ladies applying for training in the male Bydgoszcz Rowing Association forced the creation of an independent female club. On 13 March 1927 the club was registered in the Polish Association of Rowing Associations, and in the summer of the same year the ladies took a high 4th place in international regatta under the patronage of President Mościcki and gained recognition of the entire sporty Bydgoszcz. From 1928 the club settlements had sailed from victory to victory. On August 15, 1932, the four with the helmsman won the first gold medal of the Polish Championship in history. Until the outbreak of the war, gold medals in this competition were awarded four more times to the kebabs, from 1936 to 1939. In the interwar years, the Bydgoszcz rowers won a total of five titles of Polish champions, seven times they were runners, and once in the Polish championships they won third place. In 1936, and in 1938, the club won twice the Polish team championship and took the first place in the classification of women's clubs in the Polish Association of Rowing Associations. It can be said that thanks to the Bydgoszcz rowing club in the interwar period Bydgoszcz was the most important center of women's rowing throughout the Second Republic. Sports triumphs returned to the club in the post-war years. According to historical sources, by 1996 the contestants won 92 medals in national and international regattas with the rank of the Polish Championship and the World Cup. In the contemporary years, the club's medal achievements increased by further medals of the Polish, European and summer university championships. The club raised many medalists of the country, Europe, the world, even Olympics. Among them is Anna Baranowska, participant of the Olympic Games in Moscow, Agnieszka Tomczak, the Sydney Olympics, the multimedia champion of the world and Europe, the world champion Katarzyna Weinrauder, as well as the Polish and European champion Agnieszka Tomczak, Monika Kowalska, Patrycja Welnińska, Joanna Kocikowska, Paulina Grzella, Dominika Baranowska and other great competitors. These wonderful successes were possible thanks to the trainers among whom they were and are: Mieczysław Kaczmarek, Bogdan Kościelski, Ryszard Kowalik, Henryk Rutyna, Eugeniusz Nowicki, Andrzej Skowronski, Grzegorz Jankowski, Michał Plewinski and Ryszard Chojnacki. Amateur clubs are the salt of Polish sport, and water sports can shape young characters for an adult life. ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u659	Thank you very much, Member. I invite you to make a statement by Anna Dąbrowska-Banaszek.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u660	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! 82 years ago, on December 1, 1939, in Chelm, the Germans committed the first mass murder on Jews during World War II. How did this happen? On November 30, 1939, the German authorities in Chełma ordered all Jews aged 16 to 60 to appear on 1 December at 3 p.m. 8.30 in the central square of the city, en. Edward Łuczkowski. Many, fearing the worst, hid in the basements earlier - some also in the Chelm underground - or sheltered in the homes of non-Jewish friends. On 1 December 1939 almost 2 000 gathered in the centre of Chelm. Jews. Those who arrived in the square on December 1st were surrounded by armed soldiers. They were informed of the deprivation of civil rights and the conviction for expulsion from the city. The famous Chelm doctors David Walberger and Israel Ox were shot in the square. About an hour. 12.30 Jews placed in 10 columns of about 200 were ordered to march under the escort of 60 SS men towards Hrubieszow. The first collective execution of 20 people was carried out by the Germans in Borek Forest near the military hospital. Then there were more. In addition to the execution, the SS killed anyone who couldn't keep up or stop. Two villagers employed by a Chełm Jewish woman to determine the fate of her husband only along 36 km of Chełm - Hrubieszów road counted more than 600 killed. German reports indicate that 440 of the 1018 Jews were shot for trying to escape. On 2 December in Hrubieszów 800 Jews from Chełma Germany joined the group of over 1000 Hrubieszow Jews, forcing everyone to march towards the border. And although they were only 4 km to the border, Germany led them through fields, forests and wetlands along a circular route of more than 50 km, competing among themselves on 3 December for the number of Jews shot. The remaining men were sent to Sokala and Bełz. During the four-day march, Jews received only one loaf of bread per day for 30 people. On December 4, they reached Sokala. The Germans ordered them to cross the border, but the Soviet border guard initially refused to accept them. Many drowned trying to cross Bug. In the end, few Jews managed to escape with their lives. Few Chełm Jews decided to return to Chełm. According to estimates made during the war with nearly 2 thousand. gathered on 1 December 1939 About 150 men survived the death march by Edward Łuczkowski. The surrounding population was terrified to mention the sight of hundreds killed along the Chelm - Hrubieszów road. On 1 December 2019 a plaque commemorating the event was unveiled in Chelm. Today, on the 82nd anniversary of the march of death, let us bow to the reflection of our past. Let us work together so that there are no more such events. Thank you. [[Applause]]

I do not hide that I would very much like this edition to start another edition in the coming years. In today's world, there's too much evil, and it's important that we focus on the good. Media patrons of the gala are: Polish Television, TVP3 program Rzeszów, Polish Radio Rzeszów, Radio Via, Catholic Radio Zamość. On the day of their holidays, I wish all volunteers the strength and courage to face difficult challenges and satisfaction in doing good throughout the year and throughout their lives.
