ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u667	Access for disabled persons to information using electronic services. Access to information using electronic services is certainly one of the most important areas for the functioning of people with disabilities in the field of information and communication. Experts indicate that people with disabilities have problems with the use of Internet sources and interactive devices on their own. It creates, for example. difficulties in handling various types of official matters. Current technical capabilities allow for the creation of resources that are fully accessible to all audiences, including those with a disability in the visual organ and in the hearing organ. It is pointed out that public entities are gradually introducing the recommended changes in the access of disabled persons to information using electronic services. According to the available data, only 1,7% in 2013 the public administrations surveyed met the minimum requirements. In 2015, this figure developed at 13%. , reached 22.9% in 2016 , and in 2017 it was 47.8%. The trend is therefore clearly increasing. I am convinced that further work by many government institutions and bodies will increasingly improve the indicator on access for people with disabilities to information using electronic services. Thank you for the daily involvement of many people who show that supporting people with disabilities is a very important task today and involves responsibility. Together with the staff of my parliamentary office, we are helping to the extent possible and competent with regard to these activities.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u668	Parliamentary statement on investments supporting the development of tourism in Głogów. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The Odrza Bay, now known as the Neptune Bay, was created as a result of a two-year investment (1931), whose main objective was to straighten the Oder stream from the Seev Small to the height of the rowing club's buildings. Before World War II, Neptune's surroundings were vibrant and gathered residents and tourists from the region. Today, the Dolnośląskie voivodship authorities, together with the city authorities, want to return this place to the residents and tourists and develop the bay area and the Neptune building. The project envisages the construction of a river harbour at the bay along with the construction of the necessary accompanying infrastructure and the development of the area, making the area more friendly and safer for both people and nature. The project also provides for a thorough modernisation and adaptation of the currently unused building Neptune, in which the Natural and Tourist Information Centre of the Odrzański Route will be established. The facility will be adapted to the needs of people with disabilities, equipped with RES installation. Three viewing terraces and a green area with elements of small architecture will be created directly at the marina. The project also provides for the construction of an inter-city bicycle road. The project aims to ensure that all future users of the complex are taken care of - it will meet the needs of residents, visitors and athletes. Głogów will gain its attractiveness and will certainly attract tourists from all over Lower Silesia. On the tourist map Poland will appear another interesting and attractive place. The enthusiasm of the investment is not covered by self-governments from Głogów County, who with one voice support the essence and value of this project. Nearly two years from now, the Głogów region will gain a new face, and the Neptune Bay will become Głogów's card.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-12-01-sejm-43-2.u669	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! Appropriate financing of health protection is one of the key elements in terms of the possibility of providing adequate conditions for the treatment of citizens by bodies providing such assistance. That is why we were amazed to learn that the National Health Fund is behind payments to the Beskidzy Oncology Centre. These arrears, when it comes to the scale of needs, are enormous and reach several million zlotys. This is an absolutely unacceptable and harmful situation. Maintaining this condition threatens that in extreme cases the Beskidz Centre for Oncology, which provides medical services to oncology patients, will not be able to provide benefits. It's hard to imagine a worse scenario. It will put people in extreme danger. I therefore ask the Minister of Health, Mr Adam Niedzielski, to explain this situation urgently and to take decisions which will result in the transfer of all outstanding funds to the Beskidzki Oncology Centre.
